    Merck and Moderna presented full Phase 3 INTerpath-001 data (intismeran autogene + Keytruda vs. Keytruda alone, in resected Stage IIB-IV melanoma) at the ESMO 2026 Presidential Symposium in Madrid, following the Oct 22 data leak. At a median follow-up of 24.6 months: recurrence-free survival was improved, reducing the risk of recurrence or death by 42% (HR 0.58, 95% CI 0.45-0.75, two-sided p<0.001); distant metastasis-free survival was improved, reducing risk by 38% (HR 0.62, 95% CI 0.45-0.85, p=0.002). Overall survival remains immature and not yet statistically significant, though investigators reported a favorable trend. No new safety signals vs. prior studies. The companies said the data have been accepted for publication in the New England Journal of Medicine.
    source
  8. 2026-10-2222dclinical
    Shares fell 17.6% to $202.63 on unusually heavy volume after partial melanoma-vaccine data were released early
    Described as a 'sell-the-news' reaction to incomplete Phase 3 data on the Merck-partnered cancer vaccine program.source
  9. 2026-10-2222ddata leak / sell-the-news

Commentary attributed the drop to valuation/sell-the-news positioning (stock up >8x YTD before the drop) rather than the data itself; Citigroup had downgraded Moderna to Sell shortly before. Merck fell only ~2% the same day.
    -18% to -26% (sources differ) on 2026-10-22; CNBC reported the slide was still continuing as of 2026-10-26 premarket (-8%)source

  6. 2026-11-0211dregulatory
    FDA accepted uniQure's BLA for ifezuntirgene inilparvovec (AMT-130) for Huntington's disease and granted Priority Review; PDUFA target action date set for May 1, 2027. The submission relies on 36-month data from 12 high-dose patients vs. a propensity-score-matched external control; the company and FDA have also aligned on a confirmatory trial (~200 patients, 2:1 randomized to AMT-130 vs. standard of care, TFC as the 36-month primary endpoint). The FDA's acceptance letter indicates it is tentatively planning to hold an advisory committee meeting, though no date has been set.
    source
  7. 2026-11-0211dregulatory
    uniQure announced the FDA accepted its BLA for AMT-130 (ifezuntirgene inilparvovec, Huntington's disease gene therapy) under accelerated approval with Priority Review, with a confirmed PDUFA target action date of 2027-05-01 -- resolving the secondary-sourced-only date flagged in the prior pass. The FDA is tentatively planning an advisory committee meeting. uniQure and the FDA have agreed on the design of a confirmatory trial (~200 patients, randomized 2:1, primary endpoint at 36 months), with US patient screening expected to begin by year-end 2026. The UK MHRA has separately validated a parallel marketing authorization application. The filing relies on 36-month Phase 1/2 data vs. a propensity-score-matched external control from the Enroll-HD natural history database. This reverses the FDA's November 2025 position that the Phase 1/2 data were insufficient.
    source

    Edison International's Q3 2026 Form 10-Q discloses that the county fire department and CAL FIRE issued reports in August 2026 pointing to an electrical arcing event on a de-energized, idle SCE transmission line as the Eaton Fire's probable ignition source; SCE states that, absent new evidence, its equipment was likely associated with the ignition. The filing also discloses that SCE exhausted its $1.0 billion of self-insurance recoveries for Eaton Fire losses as of 2026-02-11, that fines/penalties tied to the fire will not be recoverable from insurance, the Wildfire Fund, or electric rates, and that a state safety investigation and a Los Angeles County District Attorney investigation remain open (SCE says it sees no basis for a felony liability finding). Multiple individual, subrogation, and public-entity lawsuits remain pending against SCE and Edison International.
    Clarifies the probable ignition cause and the exhaustion of self-insurance, while leaving total liability unquantified; keeps both SCE and the parent exposed to ongoing litigation and investigationssource

  5. 2026-10-1925drestatement/regulatory
    GoPro filed a preliminary proxy statement for the Starman Optical merger and simultaneously amended its FY2025 10-K and Q1/Q2 2026 10-Qs to correct non-cash accounting errors in a warrant liability (understated using too-short an expected term plus an illiquidity discount) and a convertible debenture's embedded conversion derivative. Corrections: FY2025 net loss restated to $92.6M (from $93.5M); H1 2026 net loss restated to $127.5M (from $131.8M); short-term debt at 2026-06-30 restated to $81.6M (from $72.7M). Revenue, gross margin, operating loss, cash and EBITDA are stated as unaffected. The restatement triggered a default/event of default under GoPro's credit facilities, convertible debentures and senior secured notes; the FY2025 10-K/A discloses GoPro was out of compliance with a 1.25x minimum asset-coverage ratio under its Farallon-agented credit agreement as of 2025-09-30 and 2025-12-31, with waivers subsequently obtained. Starman reiterated commitment to closing on the original terms.
    No clear same-day stock reaction identified this session; the merger continues to be described as on track for a year-end 2026 close.source

  9. 2026-09-012moM&A
    GoPro entered a definitive merger agreement with Starman Optical, a privately held optical-photonics company. Under the agreement, each eligible GoPro share converts into the right to receive 0.1 shares of the surviving corporation's common stock plus $1.14 in cash (without interest); the cash portion is subject to reduction if net working capital at the measurement date is below -$110.0 million. Midtown Equities LLC has committed up to $395.0 million in cash funding. Starman has also agreed to fully repay GoPro's ~$92 million of outstanding debt at closing. Parent is expected to own ~90% and existing GoPro stockholders ~10% of the surviving company's Class A common stock. Aggregate press-reported cash consideration is ~$285 million. The deal carries a $10 million termination fee payable by GoPro in certain circumstances and is expected to close by December 31, 2026, subject to regulatory approval and a stockholder vote.
    source
  10. 2026-09-012moM&A
    GoPro announces definitive merger agreement with Starman Optical: GoPro shareholders get an aggregate $285M cash ($1.14/share) plus ~10% of the combined company; GoPro's ~$92M debt repaid at closing; pivot toward AI/defense optics; expected to close by year-end 2026
    major re-rating event for a distressed namesource

How this list runs itself

Two passes a day

A light pre-market pass checks the movers list and any overnight news or filing on names already tracked. A full pass after the close re-researches everything that came due and rebuilds this page.

Cadence, not a ranking

Every new name starts daily. Three straight full passes with nothing new drops it to weekly; three quiet weeks drops it to monthly; three quiet months archives it. Tiers are about attention, not quality.

What counts as "something new"

A dated catalyst, a change in EPS, P/E, revenue, guidance, share count or earnings date, a material filing, an 8%+ session move, or RVOL at 3x or better. Earnings inside 14 days pin a name to daily on their own.

Gaps stay gaps

A figure that could not be established reads not disclosed rather than being inferred, and a negative-earnings company shows no P/E instead of a negative multiple.

Vs sector / vs market

Not a statistical correlation — this page holds no price history to compute one. It's the gap, in percentage points, between a name's latest session move and the same-session average move of its sector peers, and of every tracked name. Context, not a signal.

Research notes, not advice. Every figure here comes from a public source at the time of the pass and can be stale, revised, or wrong; earnings dates in particular move. Nothing on this page is a recommendation, a rating, or a price target — there is deliberately no score anywhere in it. Verify anything you would act on.
